														<h1 class="c-article__title">Hunting for Mythic in network traffic</h1>							<div class="c-article__info">
																	<p class="c-article__headline u-hidden u-block@md">

											<div class="js-reading-content">
												<div class="c-wysiwyg">
													<h2 id="post-exploitation-frameworks">Post-exploitation frameworks</h2>
<p>Threat actors frequently employ post-exploitation frameworks in cyberattacks to maintain control over compromised hosts and move laterally within the organization&#8217;s network. While they once favored closed-source frameworks, such as Cobalt Strike and Brute Ratel C4, open-source projects like Mythic, Sliver, and Havoc have surged in popularity in recent years. Malicious actors are also quick to adopt relatively new frameworks, such as Adaptix C2.</p>
<p>Analysis of popular frameworks revealed that their development focuses heavily on evading detection by antivirus and EDR solutions, often at the expense of stealth against systems that analyze network traffic. While obfuscating an agent&#8217;s network activity is inherently challenging, agents must inevitably communicate with their command-and-control servers. Consequently, an agent&#8217;s presence in the system and its malicious actions can be detected with the help of various network-based intrusion detection systems (IDS) and, of course, Network Detection and Response (NDR) solutions.</p>
<p>This article examines methods for detecting the Mythic framework within an infrastructure by analyzing network traffic. This framework has gained significant traction among various threat actors, including Mythic Likho (Arcane Wolf) и GOFFEE (Paper Werewolf), and continues to be used in APT and other attacks.</p>
<h2 id="the-mythic-framework">The Mythic framework</h2>
<p><a href="https://securelist.com/tag/mythic-framework/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Mythic C2</a> is a multi-user command and control (C&amp;C, or C2) platform designed for managing malicious agents during complex cyberattacks. Mythic is built on a Docker container architecture, with its core components – the server, agents, and transport modules – written in Python. This architecture allows operators to add new agents, communication channels, and custom modifications on the fly.</p>
<p>Since Mythic is a versatile tool for the attacker, from the defender&#8217;s perspective, its use can align with multiple stages of the Unified Kill Chain, as well as a large number of tactics, techniques, and procedures in the MITRE ATT&amp;CK® framework.</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Pivoting</strong> is a tactic where the attacker uses an already compromised system as a pivot point to gain access to other systems within the network. In this way, they gradually expand their presence within the organization&#8217;s infrastructure, bypassing firewalls, network segmentation, and other security controls.</li>
<li><strong>Collection (TA0009)</strong> is a tactic focused on gathering and aggregating information of value to the attacker: files, credentials, screenshots, and system logs. In the context of network operations, collection is often performed locally on compromised hosts, with data then packaged for transfer. Tools like Mythic automate the discovery and selection of data sought by the adversary.</li>
<li><strong>Exfiltration (TA0010)</strong> is the process of moving collected information out of the secured network via legitimate or covert channels, such as HTTP(s), DNS, or SMB, etc. Attackers may use resident agents or intermediate relays (pivot hosts) to conceal the exfiltration source and route.</li>
<li><strong>Command and Control (TA0011)</strong> encompasses the mechanisms for establishing and maintaining a communication channel between the operator and compromised hosts to transmit commands and receive status updates. This includes direct connections, relaying through pivot hosts, and the use of covert protocols. Frameworks like Mythic provide advanced C2 capabilities, such as scheduled command execution, tunneling, and multi-channel communication, which complicate the detection and blocking of their activity.</li>
</ul>
<p>This article focuses exclusively on the Command and Control (TA0011) tactic, whose techniques can be effectively detected within the network traffic of Mythic agents.</p>
<h2 id="detecting-mythic-agent-activity-in-network-traffic">Detecting Mythic agent activity in network traffic</h2>
<p>At the time of writing, Mythic supports data transfer over HTTP/S, WebSocket, TCP, SMB, DNS, and MQTT. The platform also boasts over a dozen different agents, written in Go, Python, and C#, designed for Windows, macOS, and Linux.</p>
<p>Mythic employs two primary architectures for its command network:</p>
<ul>
<li>In this model, agents communicate with adjacent agents forming a chain of connections which eventually leads to a node communicating directly with the Mythic C2 server. For this purpose, agents utilize TCP and SMB.</li>
<li>In this model, agents communicate directly with the C2 server via HTTP/S, WebSocket, MQTT, or DNS.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="p2p-communication">P2P communication</h3>
<p>Mythic provides pivoting capabilities via named SMB pipes and TCP sockets. To detect Mythic agent activity in P2P mode, we will examine their network traffic and create corresponding Suricata detection rules (signatures).</p>
<h4 id="p2p-communication-via-smb">P2P communication via SMB</h4>
<p>When managing agents via the SMB protocol, a named pipe is used by default for communication, with its name matching the agent&#8217;s UUID.</p>
<p>Although this parameter can be changed, it serves as a reliable indicator and can be easily described with a regular expression. Example:<br />
[a-z0-9]{8}\-[a-z0-9]{4}\-[a-z0-9]{4}\-[a-z0-9]{4}\-[a-z0-9]{12}</p>
<p>For SMB communication, agents encode and encrypt data according to the pattern: base64(UUID+AES256(JSON)). This data is then split into blocks and transmitted over the network. The screenshot below illustrates what a network session for establishing a connection between agents looks like in Wireshark.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118292" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1.png" alt="" width="1111" height="254" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1.png 1111w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1-300x69.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1-1024x234.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1-768x176.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1-740x169.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200718/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic1-800x183.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1111px) 100vw, 1111px" /></a></p>
<p>Commands and their responses are packaged within the <em>MythicMessage</em> data structure. This structure contains three header fields, as well as the commands themselves or the corresponding responses:</p>
<ul>
<li>Total size (4 bytes)</li>
<li>Number of data blocks (4 bytes)</li>
<li>Current block number (4 bytes)</li>
<li>Base64-encoded data</li>
</ul>
<p>The screenshot below shows an example of SMB communication between agents.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118293" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2.png" alt="" width="1052" height="325" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2.png 1052w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2-300x93.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2-1024x316.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2-768x237.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2-740x229.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2-906x280.png 906w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200749/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic2-800x247.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1052px) 100vw, 1052px" /></a></p>
<p>The agent (10.63.101.164) sends a command to another agent in the <em>MythicMessage</em> format. The first three Write Requests transmit the total message size, total number of blocks, and current block number. The fourth request transmits the Base64-encoded data. This is followed by a sequence of Read Requests, which are also transmitted in the <em>MythicMessage</em> format.</p>
<p>Below are the data transmitted in the fourth field of the <em>MythicMessage</em> structure.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118294" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3.png" alt="" width="597" height="601" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3.png 597w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3-298x300.png 298w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3-150x150.png 150w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3-348x350.png 348w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3-278x280.png 278w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200819/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic3-50x50.png 50w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 597px) 100vw, 597px" /></a></p>
<p>The content is encoded in Base64. Upon decoding, the structure of the transmitted information becomes visible: it begins with the UUID of the infected host, followed by a data block encrypted using AES-256.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200849/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic4.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118295" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200849/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic4.png" alt="" width="726" height="370" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200849/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic4.png 726w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200849/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic4-300x153.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200849/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic4-687x350.png 687w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10200849/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic4-549x280.png 549w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 726px) 100vw, 726px" /></a></p>
<p>The fact that the data starts with a UUID string can be leveraged to create a signature-based detection rule that searches network packets for the identifier pattern.</p>
<p>To search for packets containing a UUID, the following signature can be applied. It uses specific request types and protocol flags as filters (<em>Command: Ioctl (11), Function: FSCTL_PIPE_WAIT</em> <em>(0x00110018)</em>), followed by a check to see if the pipe name matches the UUID pattern.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

class="crayon-s">"/\x48\x00\x00\x00[\x00-\xFF]{2}([a-z0-9]\x00){8}\-\x00([a-z0-9]\x00){4}\-\x00([a-z0-9]\x00){4}\-\x00([a-z0-9]\x00){4}\-\x00([a-z0-9]\x00){12}$/R"</span><span class="crayon-sy">;</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-v">threshold</span><span class="crayon-o">:</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-e">type </span><span class="crayon-v">both</span><span class="crayon-sy">,</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-e">track </span><span class="crayon-v">by_src</span><span class="crayon-sy">,</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-i">count</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-cn">1</span><span class="crayon-sy">,</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-i">seconds</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-cn">60</span><span class="crayon-sy">;</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-v">reference</span><span class="crayon-o">:</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-v">url</span><span class="crayon-sy">,</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-v">https</span><span class="crayon-o">:</span><span class="crayon-c">//github.com/MythicC2Profiles/smb; classtype: ndr1; sid: 9000101; rev: 1;)</span></div></div></td>
					</tr>
				</table>
			</div>
		</div>
<!-- [Format Time: 0.0003 seconds] -->
<p>
Agent activity can also be detected by analyzing data transmitted in SMB <em>WriteRequest</em> packets with the protocol flag <em>Command: Write (9)</em> and a distinct packet structure where the <em>BlobOffset</em> and <em>BlobLen</em> fields are set to zero. If the Data field is Base64-encoded and, after decoding, begins with a UUID-formatted string, this indicates a command-and-control channel.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

<p>
Below is the KATA NDR user interface displaying an alert about detecting a Mythic agent operating in P2P mode over SMB. In this instance, the first rule – which checks the request type, protocol flags, and the UUID pattern – was triggered.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118296" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5.png" alt="" width="1175" height="1228" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5.png 1175w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-287x300.png 287w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-980x1024.png 980w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-768x803.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-335x350.png 335w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-740x773.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-268x280.png 268w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201110/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic5-800x836.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1175px) 100vw, 1175px" /></a></p>
<p>It should be noted that these signatures have a limitation. If the SMBv3 protocol with encryption enabled is used, Mythic agent activity cannot be detected with signature-based methods. A possible alternative is behavioral analysis. However, in this context, it suffers from low accuracy and a high false-positive rate. The SMB protocol is widely used by organizations for various legitimate purposes, making it difficult to isolate behavioral patterns that definitively indicate malicious activity.</p>
<h4 id="p2p-communication-via-tcp">P2P communication via TCP</h4>
<p>Mythic also supports P2P communications via TCP. The connection initialization process appears in network traffic as follows:</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118297" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6.png" alt="" width="1067" height="380" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6.png 1067w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-300x107.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-1024x365.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-768x274.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-983x350.png 983w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-740x264.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-786x280.png 786w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201156/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic6-800x285.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1067px) 100vw, 1067px" /></a></p>
<p>As with SMB, the <em>MythicMessage</em> structure is used for transmitting and receiving data. First, the data length (4 bytes) is sent as a big-endian DWORD in a separate packet. Subsequent packets transmit the number of data blocks, the current block number, and the data itself. However, unlike SMB packets, the value of the current block number field is always 0x00000000, due to TCP&#8217;s built-in packet fragmentation support.</p>
<p>The data encoding scheme is also analogous to what we observed with SMB and appears as follows: base64(UUID+AES256(JSON)). Below is an example of a network packet containing Mythic data.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201246/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic7.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118298" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201246/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic7.png" alt="" width="599" height="553" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201246/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic7.png 599w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201246/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic7-300x277.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201246/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic7-379x350.png 379w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201246/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic7-303x280.png 303w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 599px) 100vw, 599px" /></a></p>
<p>The decoded data appears as follows:</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118299" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8.png" alt="" width="795" height="446" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8.png 795w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8-300x168.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8-768x431.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8-270x150.png 270w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8-624x350.png 624w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8-740x415.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201330/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic8-499x280.png 499w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 795px) 100vw, 795px" /></a></p>
<p>Similar to communication via SMB, signature-based detection rules can be created for TCP traffic to identify Mythic agent activity by searching for packets containing UUID-formatted strings. Below are two Suricata detection rules. The first rule is a utility rule. It does not generate security alerts but instead tags the TCP session with an internal flag, which is then checked by another rule. The second rule verifies the flag and applies filters to confirm that the current packet is being analyzed at the beginning of a network session. It then decodes the Base64 data and searches the resulting content for a UUID-formatted string.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

		<div id="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-693c8b7eabdad416992176" class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-syntax crayon-theme-classic urvanov-syntax-highlighter-font-monaco urvanov-syntax-highlighter-os-pc print-yes notranslate" data-settings=" minimize scroll-mouseover wrap" style=" margin-top: 12px; margin-bottom: 12px; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">
		
			<div class="crayon-toolbar" data-settings=" mouseover overlay hide delay" style="font-size: 12px !important;height: 18px !important; line-height: 18px !important;"><span class="crayon-title"></span>
			<div class="crayon-tools" style="font-size: 12px !important;height: 18px !important; line-height: 18px !important;"><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-nums-button" title="Toggle Line Numbers"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain-button" title="Toggle Plain Code"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-wrap-button" title="Toggle Line Wrap"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-expand-button" title="Expand Code"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-copy-button" title="Copy"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-popup-button" title="Open Code In New Window"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div></div></div>
			<div class="crayon-info" style="min-height: 16.8px !important; line-height: 16.8px !important;"></div>
			<div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain-wrap"><textarea  class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain print-no" data-settings="dblclick" readonly style="-moz-tab-size:4; -o-tab-size:4; -webkit-tab-size:4; tab-size:4; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">
alert tcp any any -&gt; any any (msg: "Trojan.Mythic.TCP.C&amp;C"; flow: from_server, established; dsize: 4; stream_size: server, &lt;, 6; stream_size: client, &lt;, 3; content: "|00 00|"; depth: 2; pcre: "/^\x00\x00[\x00-\x5C]{1}[\x00-\xFF]{1}$/"; flowbits: set, mythic_tcp_p2p_msg_len; flowbits: noalert; threshold: type both, track by_src, count 1, seconds 60; reference: url, https://github.com/MythicC2Profiles/tcp; classtype: ndr1; sid: 9000103; rev: 1;)

alert tcp any any -&gt; any any (msg: "Trojan.Mythic.TCP.C&amp;C"; flow: from_server, established; dsize: &gt; 300; stream_size: server, &lt;, 6000; stream_size: client, &lt;, 6000; flowbits: isset, mythic_tcp_p2p_msg_len; content: "|00 00 00|"; depth: 3; content: "|00 00 00 00|"; distance: 1; within: 4; base64_decode: bytes 64, offset 0, relative; base64_data; pcre: "/^[a-z0-9]{8}\-[a-z0-9]{4}\-[a-z0-9]{4}\-[a-z0-9]{4}\-[a-z0-9]{12}/"; threshold: type both, track by_src, count 1, seconds 60; reference: url, https://github.com/MythicC2Profiles/tcp; classtype: ndr1; sid: 9000104; rev: 1;)</textarea></div>
			<div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-main" style="">
				<table class="crayon-table">
					<tr class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-row">
				<td class="crayon-nums " data-settings="show">

<p>
Below is the NDR interface displaying an example of the two rules detecting a Mythic agent operating in P2P mode over TCP.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118300" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9.png" alt="" width="1180" height="1230" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9.png 1180w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-288x300.png 288w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-982x1024.png 982w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-768x801.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-336x350.png 336w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-740x771.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-269x280.png 269w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201450/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic9-800x834.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1180px) 100vw, 1180px" /></a></p>
<h2 id="egress-transport-modules">Egress transport modules</h2>
<h3 id="covert-egress-communication">Covert Egress communication</h3>
<p>For stealthy operations, Mythic allows agents to be managed through popular services. This makes its activity less conspicuous within network traffic. Mythic includes transport modules based on the following services:</p>
<ul>
<li>Discord</li>
<li>GitHub</li>
<li>Slack</li>
</ul>
<p>Of these, only the first two remain relevant at the time of writing. Communication via Slack (the Slack C2 Profile transport module) is no longer supported by the developers and is considered deprecated, so we will not examine it further.</p>
<h4 id="the-discord-c2-profile-transport-module">The Discord C2 Profile transport module</h4>
<p>The use of the Discord service as a mediator for C2 communication within the Mythic framework has been gaining popularity recently. In this scenario, agent traffic is indistinguishable from normal Discord activity, with commands and their execution results masquerading as messages and file attachments. Communication with the server occurs over HTTPS and is encrypted with TLS. Therefore, detecting Mythic traffic requires decrypting this.</p>
<h5 id="analyzing-decrypted-tls-traffic">Analyzing decrypted TLS traffic</h5>
<p>Let&#8217;s assume we are using an NDR platform in conjunction with a network traffic decryption (TLS inspection) system to detect suspicious network activity. In this case, we operate under the assumption that we can decrypt all TLS traffic. Let&#8217;s examine possible detection rules for that scenario.</p>
<p>Agent and server communication occurs via Discord API calls to send messages to a specific channel. Communication between the agent and Mythic uses the MythicMessageWrapper structure, which contains the following fields:</p>
<ul>
<li>message: the transmitted data</li>
<li>sender_id: a GUID generated by the agent, included in every message</li>
<li>to_server: a direction flag – a message intended for the server or the agent</li>
<li>id: not used</li>
<li>final: not used</li>
</ul>
<p>Of particular interest to us is the <em>message</em> field, which contains the transmitted data encoded in Base64. The MythicMessageWrapper message is transmitted in plaintext, making it accessible to anyone with read permissions for messages on the Discord server.</p>
<p>Below is an example of data transmission via messages in a Discord channel.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118301" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10.png" alt="" width="1812" height="253" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10.png 1812w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-300x42.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-1024x143.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-768x107.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-1536x214.png 1536w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-740x103.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-1600x223.png 1600w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201548/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic10-800x112.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1812px) 100vw, 1812px" /></a></p>
<p>To establish a connection, the agent authenticates to the Discord server via the API call /api/v10/gateway/bot. We observe the following data in the network traffic:</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201621/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic11.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118302" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201621/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic11.png" alt="" width="723" height="114" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201621/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic11.png 723w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201621/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic11-300x47.png 300w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 723px) 100vw, 723px" /></a></p>
<p>After successful initialization, the agent gains the ability to receive and respond to commands. To create a message in the channel, the agent makes a POST request to the API endpoint /channels/&lt;channel.id&gt;/messages. The network traffic for this call is shown in the screenshot below.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201705/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic12.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118303" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201705/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic12.png" alt="" width="740" height="339" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201705/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic12.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201705/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic12-300x137.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201705/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic12-611x280.png 611w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 740px) 100vw, 740px" /></a></p>
<p>After decoding the Base64, the content of the message field appears as follows:</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201725/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic13.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118304" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201725/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic13.png" alt="" width="721" height="521" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201725/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic13.png 721w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201725/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic13-300x217.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201725/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic13-484x350.png 484w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201725/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic13-387x280.png 387w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 721px) 100vw, 721px" /></a></p>
<p>A structure characteristic of a UUID is visible at the beginning of the packet.</p>
<p>After processing the message, the agent deletes it from the channel via a DELETE request to the API endpoint /channels/{channel.id}/messages/{message.id}.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201800/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic14.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118305" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201800/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic14.png" alt="" width="727" height="114" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201800/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic14.png 727w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201800/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic14-300x47.png 300w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 727px) 100vw, 727px" /></a></p>
<p>Below is a Suricata rule that detects the agent&#8217;s Discord-based communication activity. It checks the API activity for creating HTTP messages for the presence of Base64-encoded data containing the agent&#8217;s UUID.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

<h5 id="analyzing-encrypted-tls-traffic">Analyzing encrypted TLS traffic</h5>
<p>If Discord usage is permitted on the network and there is no capability to decrypt traffic, it becomes nearly impossible to detect agent activity. In this scenario, behavioral analysis of requests to the Discord server may prove useful. Below is network traffic showing frequent TLS connections to the Discord server, which could indicate commands being sent to an agent.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201942/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic16.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118307" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201942/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic16.png" alt="" width="724" height="181" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201942/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic16.png 724w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10201942/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic16-300x75.png 300w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 724px) 100vw, 724px" /></a></p>
<p>In this case, we can use a Suricata rule to detect the frequent TLS sessions with Discord servers:</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

		<div id="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-693c8b7eabdb1799311862" class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-syntax crayon-theme-classic urvanov-syntax-highlighter-font-monaco urvanov-syntax-highlighter-os-pc print-yes notranslate" data-settings=" minimize scroll-mouseover wrap" style=" margin-top: 12px; margin-bottom: 12px; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">
		
			<div class="crayon-toolbar" data-settings=" mouseover overlay hide delay" style="font-size: 12px !important;height: 18px !important; line-height: 18px !important;"><span class="crayon-title"></span>
			<div class="crayon-tools" style="font-size: 12px !important;height: 18px !important; line-height: 18px !important;"><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-nums-button" title="Toggle Line Numbers"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain-button" title="Toggle Plain Code"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-wrap-button" title="Toggle Line Wrap"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-expand-button" title="Expand Code"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-copy-button" title="Copy"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-popup-button" title="Open Code In New Window"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div></div></div>
			<div class="crayon-info" style="min-height: 16.8px !important; line-height: 16.8px !important;"></div>
			<div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain-wrap"><textarea  class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain print-no" data-settings="dblclick" readonly style="-moz-tab-size:4; -o-tab-size:4; -webkit-tab-size:4; tab-size:4; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">
alert tcp any any -&gt; any any (msg: "NetTool.PossibleMythicDiscordEgress.TLS.C&amp;C"; flow: to_server, established; tls_sni; content: "discord.com"; nocase; threshold: type both, track by_src, count 4, seconds 420; reference: url, https://github.com/MythicC2Profiles/discord; classtype: ndr3; sid: 9000106; rev: 1;)</textarea></div>

				</td>
						<td class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-code"><div class="crayon-pre" style="font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important; -moz-tab-size:4; -o-tab-size:4; -webkit-tab-size:4; </span><span class="crayon-v">any</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-o">-&gt;</span><span class="crayon-h"> </span><span class="crayon-e">any </span><span <p>
Another method for detecting these communications involves tracking multiple DNS queries to the discord.com domain.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118308" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17.png" alt="" width="1547" height="620" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17.png 1547w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-300x120.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-1024x410.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-768x308.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-1536x616.png 1536w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-873x350.png 873w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-740x297.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-699x280.png 699w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202044/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic17-800x321.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1547px) 100vw, 1547px" /></a></p>
<p>The following rule can be applied to detect these:</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

		<div id="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-693c8b7eabdb2295633266" class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-syntax crayon-theme-classic urvanov-syntax-highlighter-font-monaco urvanov-syntax-highlighter-os-pc print-yes notranslate" data-settings=" minimize scroll-mouseover wrap" style=" margin-top: 12px; margin-bottom: 12px; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">
		
			<div class="crayon-toolbar" data-settings=" mouseover overlay hide delay" style="font-size: 12px !important;height: 18px !important; line-height: 18px !important;"><span class="crayon-title"></span>
			<div class="crayon-tools" style="font-size: 12px !important;height: 18px !important; line-height: 18px !important;"><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-nums-button" title="Toggle Line Numbers"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain-button" title="Toggle Plain Code"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-wrap-button" title="Toggle Line Wrap"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-expand-button" title="Expand Code"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-copy-button" title="Copy"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div><div class="crayon-button urvanov-syntax-highlighter-popup-button" title="Open Code In New Window"><div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-button-icon"></div></div></div></div>
			<div class="crayon-info" style="min-height: 16.8px !important; line-height: 16.8px !important;"></div>
			<div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain-wrap"><textarea  class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-plain print-no" data-settings="dblclick" readonly style="-moz-tab-size:4; -o-tab-size:4; -webkit-tab-size:4; tab-size:4; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">
alert udp any any -&gt; any 53 (msg: "NetTool.PossibleMythicDiscordEgress.DNS.C&amp;C"; content: "|01 00 00 01 00 00 00 00 00 00|"; depth: 10; offset: 2; content: "|07|discord|03|com|00|"; nocase; distance: 0; threshold: type both, track by_src, count 4, seconds 60; reference: url, https://github.com/MythicC2Profiles/discord; classtype: ndr3; sid: 9000107; rev: 1;)</textarea></div>
			<div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-main" style="">
				<table class="crayon-table">
					<tr class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-row">
				<td class="crayon-nums " data-settings="show">
					<div class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-nums-content" style="font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;"><div class="crayon-num" data-line="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-693c8b7eabdb2295633266-1">1</div></div>
				</td>
						<td class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-code"><div class="crayon-pre" style="font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important; -moz-tab-size:4; -o-tab-size:4; -webkit-tab-size:4; <p>
Below is the NDR user interface showing an example of a custom rule in operation, detecting the activity of the Discord C2 Profile transport module for a Mythic agent within encrypted traffic based on characteristic DNS queries.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18.png" alt="" width="1063" height="785" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118366" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18.png 1063w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-300x222.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-1024x756.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-768x567.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-474x350.png 474w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-740x546.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-379x280.png 379w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100458/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-18-800x591.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1063px) 100vw, 1063px" /></a></p>
<p>The proposed rule options have low accuracy and can generate a high number of false positives. Therefore, they must be adapted to the specific characteristics of the infrastructure in which they will run. <em>Threshold</em> and <em>count</em> parameters, which control the triggering frequency and time window, require tuning.</p>
<h4 id="github-c2-profile-transport-module">GitHub C2 Profile transport module</h4>
<p>GitHub&#8217;s popularity has made it an attractive choice as a mediator for managing Mythic agents. The core concept is the same as in other covert Egress communication transport modules. Communication with GitHub utilizes HTTPS. Successful operation requires an account on the target platform and the ability to communicate via API calls. The transport module utilizes the GitHub API to send comments to pre-created Issues and to commit files to a branch within a repository controlled by the attackers. In this model, the agent interacts only with GitHub: it creates and reads comments, uploads files, and manages branches. It does not communicate with any other servers. The communication algorithm via GitHub is as follows:</p>
<ol>
<li>The agent posts a comment (check-in) to a designated Issue on GitHub, intended for agents to report their results.</li>
<li>The Mythic server validates the comment, deletes it, and posts a reply in an issue designated for server use.</li>
<li>The agent creates a branch with a name matching its UUID and writes a get_tasking file to it (performs a push request).</li>
<li>The Mythic server reads the file and writes a response file to the same branch.</li>
<li>The agent reads the response file, deletes the branch, pauses, and repeats the cycle.</li>
</ol>
<h5 id="analyzing-decrypted-tls-traffic">Analyzing decrypted TLS traffic</h5>
<p>Let&#8217;s consider an approach to detecting agent activity when traffic decryption is possible.</p>
<p>Agent communication with the server utilizes API calls to GitHub. The payload is encoded in Base64 and published in plaintext; therefore, anyone who can view the repository or analyze the traffic contents can decode it.</p>
<p>Analysis of agent communication revealed that the most useful traffic for creating detection rules is associated with publishing check-in comments, creating a branch, and publishing a file.</p>
<p>During the check-in phase, the agent posts a comment to register a new agent and establish communication.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118310" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19.png" alt="" width="1186" height="417" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19.png 1186w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-300x105.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-1024x360.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-768x270.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-995x350.png 995w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-740x260.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-796x280.png 796w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202245/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic19-800x281.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1186px) 100vw, 1186px" /></a></p>
<p>The transmitted data is encoded in Base64 and contains the agent&#8217;s UUID and the portion of the message encrypted using AES-256.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118311" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20.png" alt="" width="873" height="827" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20.png 873w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20-300x284.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20-768x728.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20-369x350.png 369w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20-740x701.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20-296x280.png 296w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202318/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic20-800x758.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 873px) 100vw, 873px" /></a></p>
<p>This allows for a signature that detects UUID-formatted substrings within GitHub comment creation requests.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

<p>
Another stage suitable for detection is when the agent creates a separate branch with its UUID as the name. All subsequent relevant communication with the server will occur within this branch. Here is an example of a branch creation request:</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118312" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21.png" alt="" width="1265" height="244" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21.png 1265w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21-300x58.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21-1024x198.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21-768x148.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21-740x143.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202421/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic21-800x154.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1265px) 100vw, 1265px" /></a></p>
<p>Therefore, we can create a detection rule to identify UUID-formatted strings within branch creation requests.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

<p>
After creating the branch, the agent writes a file to it (sends a push request), which contains Base64-encoded data.</p>

The screenshot below shows how the NDR solution logs all suspicious communications using the GitHub API and subsequently identifies the Mythic agent&#8217;s activity. The result is an alert with the verdict Trojan.Mythic.HTTP.C&amp;C.</p>

<h5 id="analyzing-encrypted-tls-traffic">Analyzing encrypted TLS traffic</h5>
<p>Communication with GitHub occurs over HTTPS; therefore, in the absence of traffic decryption capability, signature-based methods for detecting agent activity cannot be applied. Let&#8217;s consider a behavioral agent activity detection approach.</p>
<p>For instance, it is possible to detect connections to GitHub servers that are atypical in frequency and purpose, originating from network segments where this activity is not expected. The screenshot below shows an example of an agent&#8217;s multiple TLS sessions. The traffic reflects the execution of several commands, as well as idle time, manifested as constant polling of the server while awaiting new tasks.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118315" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24.png" alt="" width="1060" height="659" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24.png 1060w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-300x187.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-1024x637.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-768x477.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-563x350.png 563w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-740x460.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-450x280.png 450w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202712/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic24-800x497.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1060px) 100vw, 1060px" /></a></p>
<p>Multiple TLS sessions with the GitHub service from uncharacteristic network segments can be detected using the rule presented below:</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

		<div id="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-693c8b7eabdb8606768827" class="urvanov-syntax-highlighter-syntax crayon-theme-classic urvanov-syntax-highlighter-font-monaco urvanov-syntax-highlighter-os-pc print-yes notranslate" data-settings=" minimize scroll-mouseover wrap" style=" margin-top: 12px; margin-bottom: 12px; font-size: 12px !important; line-height: 15px !important;">

alert tcp any any -&gt; any any (msg:"NetTool.PossibleMythicGitHubEgress.TLS.C&amp;C"; flow: to_server, established; tls_sni; content: "api.github.com"; nocase; threshold: type both, track by_src, count 4, seconds 60; reference: url, https://github.com/MythicC2Profiles/github; classtype: ndr3; sid: 9000111; rev: 1;)</textarea></div>

<a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118316" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25.png" alt="" width="1382" height="849" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25.png 1382w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-300x184.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-1024x629.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-768x472.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-570x350.png 570w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-740x455.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-456x280.png 456w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202816/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic25-800x491.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1382px) 100vw, 1382px" /></a></p>
<p>Additionally, multiple DNS queries to the service can be logged in the traffic.</p>
<p>This activity is detected with the help of the following rule:</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

<p>
The screenshot below shows the NDR interface with an example of the first rule in action, detecting traces of the GitHub profile activity for a Mythic agent within encrypted TLS traffic.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26.png" alt="" width="1063" height="917" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118367" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26.png 1063w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-300x259.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-1024x883.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-768x663.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-406x350.png 406w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-740x638.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-325x280.png 325w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100511/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-26-800x690.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1063px) 100vw, 1063px" /></a></p>
<p>The suggested rule options can produce false positives, so to improve their effectiveness, they must be adapted to the specific characteristics of the infrastructure in which they will run. The parameters of the <em>threshold</em> keyword – specifically the <em>count</em> and <em>seconds</em> values, which control the number of events required to generate an alert and the time window for their occurrence in NDR – must be configured.</p>
<h3 id="direct-egress-communication">Direct Egress communication</h3>
<p>The Egress communication model allows agents to interact directly with the C2 server via the following protocols:</p>
<ul>
<li>HTTP(S)</li>
<li>WebSocket</li>
<li>MQTT</li>
<li>DNS</li>
</ul>
<p>The first two protocols are the most prevalent. The DNS-based transport module is still under development, and the module based on MQTT sees little use among operators. We will not examine them within the scope of this article.</p>
<h4 id="communication-via-http">Communication via HTTP</h4>
<p>HTTP is the most common protocol for building a Mythic agent control network. The HTTP transport container acts as a proxy between the agents and the Mythic server. It allows data to be transmitted in both plaintext and encrypted form. Crucially, the metadata is not encrypted, which enables the creation of signature-based detection rules.</p>
<p>Below is an example of unencrypted Mythic network traffic over HTTP. During a GET request, data encoded in Base64 is passed in the value of the <em>query</em> parameter.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118318" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27.png" alt="" width="790" height="412" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27.png 790w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27-300x156.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27-768x401.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27-671x350.png 671w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27-740x386.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10202951/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic27-537x280.png 537w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 790px) 100vw, 790px" /></a></p>
<p>After decoding, the agent&#8217;s UUID – generated according to a specific pattern – becomes visible. This identifier is followed by a JSON object containing the key parameters of the host, collected by the agent.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118319" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28.png" alt="" width="771" height="488" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28.png 771w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28-300x190.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28-768x486.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28-553x350.png 553w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28-740x468.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203037/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic28-442x280.png 442w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 771px) 100vw, 771px" /></a></p>
<p>If data encryption is applied, the network traffic for agent communication appears as shown in the screenshot below.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118320" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29.png" alt="" width="953" height="655" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29.png 953w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29-300x206.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29-768x528.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29-509x350.png 509w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29-740x509.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29-407x280.png 407w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203111/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic29-800x550.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 953px) 100vw, 953px" /></a></p>
<p>After decrypting the traffic and decoding from Base64, the communication data reveals the familiar structure: UUID+AES256(JSON).</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118321" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30.png" alt="" width="765" height="618" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30.png 765w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30-300x242.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30-433x350.png 433w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30-740x598.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203142/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic30-347x280.png 347w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 765px) 100vw, 765px" /></a></p>
<p>Therefore, to create a detection signature for this case, we can also rely on the presence of a UUID within the Base64-encoded data in POST requests.</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->

<p>
The screenshot below shows how the NDR platform detects agent communication with the server over HTTP, generating an alert with the name Trojan.Mythic.HTTP.C&amp;C.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31.png" alt="" width="1063" height="1406" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118368" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31.png 1063w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-227x300.png 227w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-774x1024.png 774w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-768x1016.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-265x350.png 265w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-740x979.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-212x280.png 212w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/11100522/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic-31-680x900.png 680w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 1063px) 100vw, 1063px" /></a></p>
<h4 id="communication-via-https">Communication via HTTPS</h4>
<p>Mythic agents can communicate with the server via HTTPS using the corresponding transport module. In this case, data is encrypted with TLS and is not amenable to signature-based analysis. However, the activity of Mythic agents can be detected if they use the default SSL certificate. Below is an example of network traffic from a Mythic agent with such a certificate.</p>
<p><a href="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32.png" class="magnificImage"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" class="aligncenter size-full wp-image-118323" src="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32.png" alt="" width="2047" height="1263" srcset="https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32.png 2047w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-300x185.png 300w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-1024x632.png 1024w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-768x474.png 768w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-1536x948.png 1536w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-567x350.png 567w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-740x457.png 740w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-454x280.png 454w, https://media.kasperskycontenthub.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/43/2025/12/10203341/detecting-mythic-in-network-traffic32-800x494.png 800w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 2047px) 100vw, 2047px" /></a></p>
<p>For this purpose, the following signature is applied:</p><!-- Urvanov Syntax Highlighter v2.9.0 -->
